# Plan: Tackle Open Desloppify Issues

## Context
Overall score is 80.5/100 (mechanical 91%, subjective 73.5%). We want to fix the concrete open issues surfaced by the scan. There are ~650 actionable findings across mechanical detectors, plus stale subjective scores that could be re-reviewed.

This plan covers the mechanical fixes. Subjective re-review is a separate step after.

---

## Phase 1: Dead Exports Cleanup (211 findings)

Remove unused re-exports from barrel files and unused `export` keywords from individual files.

### 1a. Barrel files — trim dead re-exports (~102 exports across 23 files)

Top targets:
- `src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/hooks/index.ts` — remove 17 dead re-exports
- `src/tools/travel-between-images/components/ShotImagesEditor/hooks/index.ts` — remove 13
- `src/shared/lib/queryKeys.ts` — remove 12 individual key factory exports (consumers use `queryKeys` object)
- `src/shared/lib/preloading/index.ts` — remove 12
- `src/shared/hooks/invalidation/index.ts` — remove 9 dead type re-exports
- `src/test/test-utils.tsx` — remove 8 unused testing-library re-exports
- Remaining 18 barrel files — ~31 exports total

**Method:** For each barrel file, delete the re-export lines for dead symbols. Run `tsc` after each batch.

### 1b. Non-barrel files — remove `export` keyword (~109 exports across 51 files)

For functions/types only used within their own file, remove the `export` keyword.

Top files: `select.tsx` (4), `card.tsx` (3), `dialog.tsx` (3), types files.

**Method:** `replace_all` or targeted edits to remove `export` from dead symbols. Run `tsc` after.

## Phase 2: Quick Fixes (5 items)

### 2a. Security false positive — wontfix
`supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/index.ts` uses HMAC signature verification (lines 46-71) — correct for Stripe webhooks. Wontfix with justification.

### 2b. Dead code in videoPosterExtractor
`src/shared/utils/videoPosterExtractor.ts` — both `extractVideoPosterFrame()` and `extractVideoFinalFrame()` are unused (zero importers). Delete the file or remove export keywords.

### 2c. Import cycle — type-only
`VideoLightbox.tsx` ↔ `useVideoLightboxRenderModel.tsx` — the import at line 12 of `useVideoLightboxRenderModel.tsx` is already `import type`. Check if the cycle detector is seeing a runtime import elsewhere, or if this is already a false positive.

### 2d. Deprecated PairMetadata type
`src/shared/components/segmentSettingsMigration.ts` line 31 — 2 importers. Low priority, skip unless quick.

### 2e. Duplicate functions
`src/shared/lib/invokeWithTimeout.ts` — the explorer found no actual duplicate file. May be a false positive. Verify and wontfix if so.

## Phase 3: Dead Functions (24 findings, high false-positive rate)

The dead function detector has confirmed false positives (e.g., `addCorruptionEvent` used in 13+ places). Need to verify each one individually before removing.

**Method:** For each of the 24 findings, grep for usage. Only remove truly dead functions. Skip test file findings entirely.

Production files to check (~14, excluding test files):
- `src/integrations/supabase/utils/timeline.ts:6`
- `src/pages/Home/components/InstallInstructionsModal.tsx:47`
- `src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useReferenceResourceMutations.ts:29`
- `src/shared/hooks/use-voice-recording.ts:20`
- `src/shared/hooks/useClickRipple.ts:13`
- `src/shared/hooks/useSubmitButtonState.ts:48`
- `src/shared/hooks/useTaskLog.ts:43`
- `src/shared/hooks/useVideoScrubbing.ts:93`
- `src/shared/lib/generationTransformers.ts:265`
- `src/shared/lib/maskGeneration.ts:24`
- `src/shared/utils/createLineageGif.ts:44`
- `src/tools/travel-between-images/components/FinalVideoSection.tsx:38`
- `src/tools/travel-between-images/components/VideoGallery/hooks/useMobileVideoPreload.ts:46`
- `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/useVideoMetadata.ts:23`

## Phase 4: Subjective Re-review

After mechanical fixes, re-review the lowest subjective dimensions to see if scores have improved. All 19 dimensions are from the same holistic review and none are marked stale.

Lowest dimensions to re-review:
- test_strategy (57), incomplete_migration (62), ai_generated_debt (65)
- abstraction_fitness (68), api_surface_coherence (69), initialization_coupling (69)
- logic_clarity (70), mid/low_level_elegance (70), high_level_elegance (72)

Split across parallel subagent reviews, then import results.

## Verification
1. `npx tsc --noEmit` — zero errors after each phase
2. `desloppify scan --path .` — rescan after all mechanical fixes
3. Compare overall score before/after

**Summary of assessment:**

The overall test_strategy score of **72** reflects a codebase that has reasonable test coverage for most critical paths, but with specific gaps:

- **Positive:** The error handling system (`errors.ts`, `handleError`) has thorough integration-level tests. The Supabase bootstrap (`createSupabaseClient`, `initializeSupabaseRuntime`, `fetchWithTimeout`) all have dedicated test files. The `utils.ts` utility functions have solid edge-case coverage. The `button.tsx` component has tests. No snapshot overuse was detected.

- **Gaps:** The Supabase client singleton caching logic (143 importers, HMR-sensitive) is entirely untested. The button tests are coupled to CSS implementation details rather than behavior. The error presentation sub-modules lack direct unit tests. The most-imported utility function (`cn`) has zero test coverage. These are not critical failures but represent governance risks for a production application -- foundational modules that could break silently.</result>

## Summary

After reading all 30 files plus `package.json` and performing targeted dependency searches across the codebase, I found four dependency health issues:

**Score: 78/100**

The codebase is generally well-managed in terms of dependencies. It uses appropriate libraries for their purposes (`@tanstack/react-query`, `@supabase/supabase-js`, `@base-ui-components/react`, `@dnd-kit`, `konva`/`react-konva`, etc.) and avoids over-reliance on heavy utility libraries like lodash. There are no circular dependency cycles visible in the reviewed files, and no version conflicts.

The four findings are:

1. **`next-themes` is effectively dead** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ui/sonner.tsx`): Imported and called but with no `ThemeProvider` mounted, the `useTheme()` hook does nothing. The app uses its own `useDarkMode` hook. This dependency can be removed entirely.

2. **`@types/jszip` is a redundant stub** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/package.json`): JSZip ships its own TypeScript definitions. The `@types/jszip` package is literally a no-op stub pointing back to jszip's own types.

3. **`uuid` for a single call site** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useHuggingFaceUpload.ts`): The package is used in exactly one file, while `crypto.randomUUID()` (the native equivalent) is already used elsewhere in the codebase (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/taskCreation.ts`).

4. **Duplicate relative time formatting** (multiple files): `date-fns`'s `formatDistanceToNow` is used in some files while hand-rolled implementations (`getTimeAgo`, `formatProcessingDuration`, `formatCompletedTime`) exist in others, creating inconsistent output formats and duplicated logic for the same purpose.</result>

## Summary of Review

I read all 20 files across the three requested dimensions. Here is the full assessment and findings.

### Scores

- **logic_clarity**: 7/10 -- Most logic is clean and straightforward, but there are several instances of dead branches, empty effect hooks, and side effects in render.
- **type_safety**: 7/10 -- Types are generally well-used, but there are unused interfaces, unused parameters in public APIs, and non-null assertions that bypass safety.
- **initialization_coupling**: 6/10 -- Two singletons are created at module scope (one accesses DOM APIs in its constructor), and the router tree is built at import time with eager component imports.

### Findings Detail

**LC-001** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/platformInstall/helpers.ts`, lines 97-105): `getInstallMethod` has three separate branches that all return `'none'` -- the firefox desktop check, the `isDesktopChromium` check, and the default fallthrough. These conditionals have no observable effect.

**LC-002** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useUpdatingTimestamp.ts`, lines 81-84): A `useEffect` with an empty body (debug logging was apparently removed but the hook was left). It still runs on every `updateTrigger` change, adding unnecessary React overhead.

**LC-003** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useTasks.ts`, lines 352-357): `usePaginatedTasks` calls `query.refetch()` and mutates a ref directly during the render phase rather than inside a `useEffect`. This violates React's no-side-effects-in-render rule and can cause double-fetches in StrictMode.

**LC-004** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useUpdatingTimestamp.ts`, line 51): The regex `.replace(/days?/, 'days')` matches singular `'day'` and replaces it with `'days'` (incorrect pluralization), and matches `'days'` replacing it with `'days'` (no-op). The singular case is handled by an earlier early-return, so this is effectively dead but misleading code.

**LC-005** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/join-clips/pages/JoinClipsPage.tsx`, line 287): Inside the `if (shouldShowSkeleton)` block, a ternary checks `hasValidData` which is necessarily `false` at that point (since `shouldShowSkeleton = isLoadingOrFetching && !hasValidData`). The true-branch of the ternary is unreachable.

**TS-001** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/generationTaskBridge.ts`, lines 25-35): `GenerationTaskMapping` and `TaskGenerationMapping` interfaces are defined but never used anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep). Dead type declarations.

**TS-002** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/edit-images/hooks/useInlineEditState.ts`, line 398): The `_onClose` parameter is accepted but never used in the function body. The underscore prefix was used to silence the TypeScript unused-variable warning. Callers (InlineEditView.tsx) still pass it, suggesting a vestigial API surface.

**TS-003** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useTasks.ts`, lines 103, 130): `buildCountQuery` and `buildDataQuery` use non-null assertions (`effectiveProjectId!`) on a nullable type. While the query is guarded by `enabled: !!effectiveProjectId`, the inner functions themselves have no type-level guarantee.

**IC-001** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/VisibilityManager.ts`, lines 64-74, 268): The `VisibilityManagerImpl` constructor accesses `document.hidden` and `document.visibilityState` directly and calls `this.initialize()` which adds DOM event listeners. The singleton is created at module scope (line 268), so any import of this module in an environment without a DOM (SSR, Node.js tests) will throw.

**IC-002** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/realtime/DataFreshnessManager.ts`, line 354): Singleton instantiated at module scope. While the constructor is safe (no browser APIs), the pattern creates implicit shared state across all 10+ importers and makes test isolation harder.

**IC-003** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/app/routes.tsx`, lines 30, 57): Environment variables are read and the entire router tree (including eager component imports for 5 tool pages) is constructed at module scope. This couples route configuration to import-time resolution, reducing code-splitting effectiveness for eager routes.</result>

## Summary of Key Findings

### Logic Clarity (score: 7.5)

The codebase has generally clear logic, but I found five issues:

1. **LOGIC-001** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/generationTransformers.ts`, lines 468-472): In `transformVariant`, the `else if (storedContentType === 'image' || !isVideo)` condition is always true when reached because the preceding `if` already handled the `isVideo === true` case. This is a tautological branch that reads as if there could be an unhandled path when there cannot be.

2. **LOGIC-002** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/toolSettingsService.ts`, lines 106, 219-221): `getUserWithTimeout` was refactored to always return `error: null`, but the caller still checks `if (authError || !user)` -- the `authError` portion is dead code.

3. **LOGIC-003** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/TaskDetails/VideoTravelDetails.tsx`, line 392): `phaseConfig.phases.length` accessed without optional chaining, inconsistent with line 380 which uses `phaseConfig?.phases`.

4. **LOGIC-004** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/travelBetweenImages/payloadBuilder.ts`, lines 141, 155): `?? undefined` is a no-op that serves no purpose, inconsistent with the conditional spread pattern used elsewhere in the same function.

5. **LOGIC-005** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/join-clips/lib/clipInitService.ts`, lines 165, 190-213): The `type` discriminator in `PendingClipAction` is set but completely ignored by the consumer function.

### Type Safety (score: 7.0)

The most significant type safety gaps are in the Supabase edge functions:

1. **TYPE-001** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/storage.ts`, lines 169-183): `verifyFileExists` declares `Promise<...>` return type but returns plain objects synchronously from a non-`async` function.

2. **TYPE-002** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`, lines 34-39): Property access on `params: unknown` without type assertion after an `object` type guard.

3. **TYPE-003** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/rateLimit.ts`, line 57): `supabaseAdmin: unknown` parameter with `.rpc()` calls.

4. **TYPE-004** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/storage.ts`): All five functions accept `supabase: unknown`, losing type safety for all storage operations.

### Initialization Coupling (score: 8.5)

The codebase handles initialization coupling reasonably well with intentional singleton patterns:

1. **INIT-001** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/integrations/supabase/client.ts`): Import-time Supabase client creation with window instrumentation -- intentional but creates a boot-order dependency.

2. **INIT-002** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/toolSettingsService.ts`): Module-level caches with an ordering dependency on `setCachedUserId`.

3. **INIT-003** (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/timelineWriteQueue.ts`): Module-level mutable Maps with no test reset mechanism, unlike the pattern established in `toolSettingsService.ts`.</result>

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify**: Custom code quality scanning tool at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/` with mechanical detectors (exports, dead code, cycles) and subjective dimensions scored by LLM review
   - **Score types**: overall (weighted blend), objective/mechanical, strict (includes subjective), verified_strict
   - **Score formula**: overall = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective
   - **Review workflow**: `desloppify review --prepare` generates investigation batches → agents review code → produce JSON with assessments + findings → `desloppify review --import findings.json` → `desloppify scan` to see updated scores
   - **findings.json format**: `{ "batch": "...", "assessments": { dimension: score }, "findings": [{ dimension, identifier, summary, related_files, evidence, suggestion, reasoning, confidence }] }`
   - **Investigation batches**: Groups of files assigned to specific dimensions for review; batches are independent and parallelizable

All four video files are present and accounted for. The video loads fine — it just takes a moment because:

1. **Large files** — the intro video is 43MB, the loop is 51MB
2. **Sequential loading** — the poster loads first, then Video A starts playing, then Video B preloads in the background
3. **Painterly reveal** — the video is hidden behind a mask animation until the poster is ready (`posterLoaded` triggers the reveal)

So the delay you see is the normal loading sequence: poster image loads → reveal animation plays → video streams in and starts playing. On slower connections the gap between poster and video playback can be noticeable.

> AGENT

It's a **painterly blob reveal** — like an ink blot expanding outward:

- **11 overlapping radial-gradient circles** are used as a CSS mask on the video container
- They're clustered slightly toward the **bottom-right (~62% 58%)** so the reveal originates from that area and sweeps outward
- Each blob has a different size multiplier (0.48x to 1.0x of `--blob-r`), so they expand at different rates — this creates an **organic, irregular edge** rather than a clean circle
- The first blob has a solid core (3-stop gradient) to prevent semi-transparency at the center during mid-animation
- `--blob-r` animates from **0 to 120** over **1.4 seconds** with an ease-out curve (`cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)`) — fast start, gentle finish
- After the animation completes, the JS handler removes the mask entirely so it doesn't interfere with the video

The overall effect: the video appears to bloom outward from slightly off-center in an organic, paint-splatter shape. Browsers without `@property` support get a simple opacity fade fallback instead.

| Dimension | Score | Gap to 95 | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|

**The realistic path to 95:**

1. **Duplication (63.5%)** — This is the single biggest lever. The 2526 clusters are mostly boilerplate patterns (similar hook structures, query patterns, etc.). Extracting shared utilities and deduplicating repeated patterns could move this 15-20 points, which alone would add ~3-4 points to strict.

2. **Type Safety (67%)** — Most findings are `unknown` casts in edge functions. Adding proper types to ~6 functions is a focused fix that could push this to 85+.

3. **Logic Clarity (73%)** — 8 specific findings: dead branches, no-op expressions, always-true conditions. Each is a surgical 1-5 line fix.

4. **AI Generated Debt (76%)** — Strip restating JSDoc, remove emoji comments, dedupe copy-pasted validation. Tedious but mechanical.

5. **Abstraction Fit (77%)** — The hardest one. Requires breaking up god components and reducing prop counts. High risk of breaking things.

**Honest assessment:** Getting from 84 to 90 is doable with focused work on type safety, logic clarity, and AI debt (the surgical fixes). Getting to 95 requires tackling duplication and abstraction fitness, which are larger structural refactors. Want me to start with the quick wins (type safety + logic clarity)?

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify**: Custom code quality tool at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/` with mechanical detectors and subjective LLM-reviewed dimensions
   - **Score formula**: overall = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective; strict includes subjective
   - **Review workflow**: `desloppify review --prepare` → agents review code → produce findings.json → `desloppify review --import findings.json` → `desloppify scan`
   - **findings.json format**: `{ "batch": "...", "assessments": { dimension: score }, "findings": [{ dimension, identifier, summary, related_files, evidence, suggestion, reasoning, confidence }] }`
   - **Current scores** (as of last scan): strict 85.7, objective 94.3, verified 93.6

3. Files and Code Sections:

   **Desloppify tool fixes (in /user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/):**
   
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/file_discovery.py`
     - Fixed `.venv*` directories not being excluded from scans. `.venv-debug` was slipping through because only `.venv` (exact match) was in DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS.
     - Added fnmatch glob support to `matches_exclusion()` and changed `".venv"` to `".venv*"` in DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS
     ```python
     def matches_exclusion(rel_path: str, exclusion: str) -> bool:
         parts = Path(rel_path).parts
         if exclusion in parts:
             return True
         if "*" in exclusion:
             import fnmatch
             if any(fnmatch.fnmatch(part, exclusion) for part in parts):
                 return True
         if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
             normalized = exclusion.rstrip("/").rstrip(os.sep)
             return rel_path.startswith(normalized + "/") or rel_path.startswith(normalized + os.sep)
         return False
     ```

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/detectors/jscpd_adapter.py`
     - The jscpd duplication detector was scanning `.venv*` directories because its `--ignore` flag didn't exclude them
     - Changed `--ignore` from `"**/node_modules/**,**/.git/**,**/__pycache__/**"` to `"**/node_modules/**,**/.git/**,**/__pycache__/**,**/.venv*/**,**/venv/**"`
     - Result: Duplication score jumped from 63.5% to 82.0% (+18.5 points)

     ```css
     @property --reveal {
       syntax: "<number>";
       inherits: false;
       initial-value: 0;
     }
     .video-reveal {
       --reveal: 0;
       -webkit-mask-image: linear-gradient(to right,
         transparent calc(50% - var(--reveal) * 0.55vmax - 5vmax),
         black       calc(50% - var(--reveal) * 0.55vmax),
         black       calc(50% + var(--reveal) * 0.55vmax),
         transparent calc(50% + var(--reveal) * 0.55vmax + 5vmax));
       /* duplicate for mask-image */
     }
     .video-reveal.revealing {
       animation: side-reveal 3s cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) forwards;
     }
     @keyframes side-reveal {
       from { --reveal: 0; }
       to   { --reveal: 100; }
     }
     ```

   - **desloppify `ignore` command crash**: `NameError: name 'TypedDict' is not defined` in `narrative/core.py`. Worked around by directly editing state JSON (which didn't persist across rescans). Then fixed the root cause in `jscpd_adapter.py` and `file_discovery.py`.
   - **State-level ignore didn't persist**: Setting findings to `status: 'ignored'` in state JSON was overwritten on next scan because jscpd re-detected the files. Fixed by updating the jscpd `--ignore` flag and the `DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS` glob.
   - **User feedback**: "Why aren't you fixing them in desloppify?" — user explicitly wanted root-cause fixes in the desloppify tool, not workarounds.
   - **Subjective scores not moving after code fixes**: Expected behavior — desloppify's subjective scores come from LLM reviews, not mechanical detection. Code fixes need to be followed by re-review to update scores.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Duplication score drag**: Identified that 1436 of 2526 duplication findings were in `.venv-debug/` (vendor code). Fixed the root cause in desloppify's jscpd adapter and file discovery, moving duplication from 63.5% to 82.0%.
   - **Type Safety**: Fixed all 8 findings by adding proper interfaces and removing `unknown` casts across edge functions and hooks.
   - **Logic Clarity**: Fixed 8 high-priority and several medium-priority logic issues (dead branches, no-ops, unreachable code).
   - **AI Generated Debt**: Fixed 10 issues via subagent (restating JSDoc, empty stubs, duplicated blocks, verbose comments).
   - **Score plateau at 85.7**: All code fixes are done but subjective scores haven't been re-reviewed. Need to launch review agents to re-evaluate all dimensions below 90.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify**: Custom code quality tool at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/` with mechanical detectors and subjective LLM-reviewed dimensions
   - **Score formula**: strict = blend of objective (mechanical) and subjective scores. Current: objective 94.3, subjective avg 84.7, strict 89.9
   - **Review workflow**: `desloppify review --prepare` → agents review code → produce findings.json → `desloppify review --import findings.json` → `desloppify scan`
   - **findings.json format**: `{ "batch": "...", "assessments": { dimension: score }, "findings": [{ dimension, identifier, summary, related_files, evidence, suggestion, reasoning, confidence }] }`
   - **To reach 95 strict**: Need subjective average of ~95.5, meaning every dimension needs to be ~96. Total points needed across all dimensions: ~205

     - After: Clean ButtonProps extending VariantProps directly, no normalization:
     ```typescript
     export interface ButtonProps
       extends React.ButtonHTMLAttributes<HTMLButtonElement>,
         VariantProps<typeof buttonVariants> {
       asChild?: boolean
     }
     const Button = React.forwardRef<HTMLButtonElement, ButtonProps>(
       ({ className, variant, size, asChild = false, ...props }, ref) => {
         const Comp = asChild ? Slot : "button"
         return (
           <Comp
             className={cn(buttonVariants({ variant, size, className }))}
             ref={ref}
             {...props}
           />
         )
       }
     )
     ```

     ```typescript
     const TABLET_UA_RE = /iPad|Tablet|Android(?!.*Mobile)|Silk|Kindle|PlayBook/i;
     const hasCoarsePointer = (): boolean => { ... };
     const isIpadOsLike = (): boolean =>
       navigator.platform === 'MacIntel' && (navigator.maxTouchPoints || 0) > 1;
     const isTabletUA = (): boolean =>
       TABLET_UA_RE.test(navigator.userAgent || '');
     ```

4. **elegance::install_modal_hardcoded_colors** (medium confidence) - 92 instances of hardcoded `gray-*` Tailwind classes in browser mockup illustrations. Somewhat defensible as decorative external-UI depictions, but won't adapt to dark mode.

## Scores

| Dimension | Previous | Current | Delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| abstraction_fitness | 77 | 81 | +4 |
| mid_level_elegance | 80 | 83 | +3 |
| low_level_elegance | 82 | 84 | +2 |

## Key Findings

**Issues identified (10):**

1. **pane-strategy-skeleton** (abstraction_fitness, high confidence) - `BasePanePositionStrategy` is a 15-line abstract class with a one-liner concrete method. The class hierarchy adds ceremony for no value.

2. **button-group-props-passthrough** (abstraction_fitness, medium confidence) - `useButtonGroupProps` is a 180-line hook that purely redistributes 25 parameters into four sub-objects with zero transformation logic.

3. **inline-edit-state-surface-area** (abstraction_fitness, high confidence) - `useInlineEditState` returns a 60-field flat object, defeating the purpose of its good internal sub-hook decomposition.

4. **get-cta-text-icon-long-params** (abstraction_fitness, high confidence) - `getCtaText` and `getCtaIcon` take 7 positional parameters each, inconsistent with the context-object pattern used by adjacent functions in the same file.

5. **media-gallery-prop-sprawl** (mid_level_elegance, high confidence) - `MediaGallery` receives ~45 props and passes ~50 to `MediaGalleryGrid`. This is the largest integration surface concern in the reviewed files.

6. **lightbox-hook-chain-depth** (mid_level_elegance, medium confidence) - ImageLightbox/VideoLightbox chain 4-5 custom hook layers, each passing the full ReturnType of the previous layer.

7. **video-lightbox-props-flat-spread** (mid_level_elegance, high confidence) - VideoLightboxProps uses 35+ flat props while ImageLightbox already groups them into sub-interfaces. The two should be aligned.

8. **image-gen-iife-params-assembly** (low_level_elegance, high confidence) - `createImageGenerationTask` uses nested IIFEs with spread to assemble params, duplicating lora map construction and obfuscating the final object shape.

9. **updating-timestamp-dead-effect** (low_level_elegance, high confidence) - `useUpdatingTimestamp` has an empty `useEffect` that was stripped of its debug logging body but not removed.

10. **tasks-hook-refetch-in-render** (mid_level_elegance, high confidence) - `usePaginatedTasks` calls `query.refetch()` during render rather than in a `useEffect`, which is an anti-pattern.

**Positive patterns recognized (8):**

- `DataFreshnessManager` - Clean singleton with graduated polling, circuit breaker, and subscriber pattern
- `generationTaskBridge` - Good consolidation of previously-scattered logic with batch support
- `useDebouncedSettingsSave` - Well-factored lifecycle management with edit version tracking
- `useProcessingTimestamp` - Balanced DRY extraction of shared timestamp mechanics
- `useLoraSubmission` - Clean functional decomposition of a complex submission flow
- `JoinClipsPage` - Follows the documented tool pattern well with clean hook/layout separation
- `VisibilityManager` - Proper centralization of visibility listeners with subscriber model
- `useGenerationMutations` - Good separation of query/mutation concerns with thorough optimistic updates</result>

<result>The assessment has been written to `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/findings-batch6.json`.

Here is a summary of the scoring rationale:

**error_consistency: 90 (was 85)** -- The error handling system is well-structured with a clean hierarchy (AppError -> NetworkError/AuthError/ValidationError/ServerError/SilentError), a proper normalize -> log -> toast pipeline, and strong adoption (468 call sites across 226 files). The main concern is `navigator.onLine` usage inside `categorizeError`, which is a side-effect in what should be a pure function.

**authorization_consistency: 92 (was 88)** -- Every non-webhook edge function uses `authenticateRequest` from the shared auth module. The auth module supports service role keys, JWTs, and PATs with proper security (rejects JWTs with service_role claims). The gap is that ownership verification (`verifyTaskOwnership`/`verifyShotOwnership`) is only used in 3-4 functions. Also, `supabaseAdmin` is typed as `unknown` in auth.ts, unlike rateLimit.ts which defines a proper interface.

**convention_outlier: 84 (was 82)** -- The `retro` and `retro-secondary` button variants use 14+ hardcoded hex colors, directly violating the "never hardcode colors" convention. Legacy `lala` variant compatibility adds unnecessary indirection. Otherwise, file organization and patterns are clean.

**naming_quality: 88 (was 82)** -- Naming is strong throughout: error classes, auth helpers, billing utilities all use clear domain-appropriate names. The only questionable name is `lala` in the legacy button variant mapping.

**contract_coherence: 82 (was 72)** -- Significant improvement. The error system's contracts are well-defined. Key issues found: `getTaskUserId` returns `'system'` as a fallback userId rather than null (violating its own return type contract), `verifyFileExists` doesn't actually verify existence (getPublicUrl always succeeds), and rate limiting fails open by design (documented but worth noting).

**dependency_health: 85 (was 75)** -- shots.ts is a clean barrel re-export. The error handling system has clear module boundaries. The Supabase client's window coupling is pragmatic but uses untyped globals. Overall the dependency graph is much healthier.

**initialization_coupling: 86 (was 78)** -- The Supabase client initialization has explicit ordering with HMR protection. The error system layers cleanly without circular dependencies. No temporal coupling issues detected.

**test_strategy: 78 (was 62)** -- 895 test files in src/ and 39 in supabase/ represent substantial coverage. The examined tests (useAutoSaveSettings, useDebouncedSettingsSave) are thorough with race condition coverage, async patterns, and proper fake timer usage. Minor issues: stale mock paths (`@/shared/lib/errorHandler` vs actual `@/shared/lib/errorHandling/handleError`) and inconsistent mock path conventions between test files. Edge function test coverage could be expanded.</result>

<result>All four files look correct. The TypeScript compiler passed with no errors. Here is a summary of all changes:

---

## Summary of Changes

### 1. VariantBadge -- deduplicated two identical JSX blocks
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/VariantBadge.tsx`

The component had two nearly-identical JSX return paths: one for `variant === 'overlay'` and one for `variant === 'inline'`. The only differences were:
- Container: `absolute gap-0.5` vs `gap-1`, plus `position` class and `style={{ zIndex }}` for overlay
- StatusBadge `size`: `"sm"` for overlay, `"md"` for inline
- Count badge background: `bg-black/60` for overlay, `bg-black/50` for inline

Consolidated into a single return block using an `isOverlay` boolean to conditionally select the differing props/classes. Reduced the component from 170 lines to 136 lines, eliminating ~35 lines of duplicated JSX.

### 2. calculateTaskResolution -- removed legacy overload
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/imageGeneration.ts`

The function had three declarations: the clean `options` signature, a deprecated positional `(projectId, customResolution?, modelName?)` signature, and the runtime implementation that handled both. Removed the deprecated overload and the runtime type-switching (`typeof projectIdOrOptions === 'string'`), leaving only the clean `CalculateTaskResolutionOptions` signature. Also updated the one internal caller (`createImageGenerationTask` at the former line 305) to use the options-object form instead of the positional form.

### 3. cacheUtils SHOTS_CACHE_VARIANTS -- improved documentation
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/shots/cacheUtils.ts`

The constant was already extracted into a named constant, but the comment was terse. Expanded the JSDoc to clearly document each value's purpose and added a maintenance note explaining that any new `useListShots` call with a different `maxImagesPerShot` value must be added here. Also exported the constant so other files can reference it if needed rather than hardcoding their own lists.

### 4. useLineageChain -- consolidated duplicate traversal logic
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useLineageChain.ts`

The file had two functions with duplicated chain-traversal logic: `fetchLineageChain` (fetches full variant data, builds `LineageItem[]`) and `getLineageDepth` (fetches only `params`, counts ancestors). Both walked the same `source_variant_id` chain with identical loop structure, visited-set cycle protection, and error handling. Consolidated `getLineageDepth` to reuse `fetchLineageChain`, computing depth as `chain.length - 1`. The minor extra data fetched per hop is negligible, and the single traversal implementation eliminates the risk of the two diverging. The external API is unchanged -- the one caller in `useVariantActions.ts` continues to work identically.

### Verification
`npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly with zero errors.</result>

<result>Good -- the import path is consistent.

---

Here is a summary of all changes made:

## 1. useSmartPolling stale closure fix

**File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useSmartPolling.ts`**

The bug was a stale closure in the `useEffect` cleanup function. The original code captured `throttleTimeoutRef.current` into a local variable `throttleTimeout` at effect setup time (line 71), then used that stale snapshot in the cleanup function (line 91). Even if `throttleTimeoutRef.current` changed during the effect's lifetime, the cleanup would only see the value from setup time.

On closer inspection, the `throttleTimeoutRef` was never actually written to anywhere in the hook -- it was vestigial dead code from a previous throttling implementation that was replaced by the `queueMicrotask` + `pendingUpdateRef` approach. The cleanup was always a no-op.

Additionally, there was no guard against calling `forceUpdate()` from a queued microtask after the effect had been cleaned up (e.g., when `queryKeyString` or `debug` changes trigger effect re-execution).

**Changes:**
- Removed the dead `throttleTimeoutRef` ref and its stale capture
- Added an `active` flag pattern: the microtask callback checks `if (active)` before calling `forceUpdate()`, and the cleanup sets `active = false`, preventing stale updates after the effect is torn down

## 2. ProjectImageSettings deprecated properties

**File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/types.ts`**

The `ProjectImageSettings` interface had 9 deprecated properties. After analyzing all usages:

- **`projectPrompts`**: Zero callers in any source file. **Removed from the type.**
- **`selectedLorasByTextModel`**: Had a lazy fallback read in `useGenerationSource.ts`. **Migrated to an explicit one-time migration** (see below), and the fallback was removed. The property remains on the type (marked deprecated) because the migration code needs to read/write it.
- **`selectedReferenceId`**, **`styleReferenceImage`**, **`styleReferenceImageOriginal`**, **`styleReferenceStrength`**, **`subjectStrength`**, **`subjectDescription`**, **`inThisScene`**: All used exclusively by migration code in `useReferenceStructureMigration.ts` (which reads them to build a migrated reference and then clears them to `undefined`). These must remain on the type for the migration code to function. No action needed.

**New file: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/legacyMigrations/useLoraCategoryMigration.ts`**

A new one-time migration hook that converts the old `selectedLorasByTextModel` (per-text-model LoRA selections) to `selectedLorasByCategory` (per-category: `'qwen'` | `'z-image'`). It:
- Only runs when `selectedLorasByTextModel` exists and `selectedLorasByCategory` doesn't
- Merges LoRAs from all qwen-family models into the `'qwen'` category, deduped by ID
- Writes the new `selectedLorasByCategory` and clears `selectedLorasByTextModel` to `undefined`
- Uses a per-project ref guard to prevent duplicate runs

**File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useLegacyMigrations.ts`**

Added the `useLoraCategoryMigration` hook call.

**File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ImageGenerationForm/hooks/useGenerationSource.ts`**

Removed the `selectedLorasByTextModel` fallback from the LoRA initialization effect (lines 121-122) and from its dependency array (line 132). The new explicit migration in `useLoraCategoryMigration` now handles converting old data before `useGenerationSource` initialization runs.

All changes pass `npx tsc --noEmit` with zero errors.</result>

<result>Good - it is used in GenerationTaskContext. Now let me verify that `consumePendingJoinClips` allocates all actions as 'fill' for first and 'append' for rest, but then `applyPendingClipActions` ignores `type` entirely:

Looking at the code again in `clipInitService.ts`:
- Line 165: `actions.push({ type: filledCount === 0 ? 'fill' : 'append', clip });` - only the first gets 'fill', rest get 'append'
- But `applyPendingClipActions` on line 190-213 doesn't actually use `action.type` - it just always tries to find an empty slot first, then appends. So the `type` field is unused.

Let me verify the `extractTaskId` function handling of the string case in `generationTransformers.ts`:

The function at line 241-246 only handles `string[]` but the type signature says `string[] | string | null`. For a plain `string`, `Array.isArray` returns false, so it returns `null` - a plain string task ID would be silently dropped.

<result>All four fixes are in place and `npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly. Here is a summary of what was changed:

---

## Summary of Changes

### 1. `supabaseAdmin` typed as `unknown` -- Fixed
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/_shared/auth.ts`

Added an import of the `SupabaseClient` type from the same `@supabase/supabase-js` version already used by other edge functions (e.g., `update-task-status/cancellationBilling.ts`):

```typescript
import type { SupabaseClient } from 'https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2.39.7';
```

Changed all four function signatures from `supabaseAdmin: unknown` to `supabaseAdmin: SupabaseClient`:
- `authenticateRequest`
- `verifyTaskOwnership`
- `verifyShotOwnership`
- `getTaskUserId`

### 2. `createGeneration` returns `Record<string, unknown>` -- Fixed
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useGenerationMutations.ts`

Changed the return type of `createGeneration` from `Promise<Record<string, unknown>>` to `Promise<GenerationRow>`. The `GenerationRow` type was already imported in the file. The function does a Supabase `.insert().select().single()` on the `generations` table and returns the resulting row, which matches the `GenerationRow` interface.

### 3. Dead interfaces in `generationTaskBridge.ts` -- Removed
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/generationTaskBridge.ts`

Deleted the unused `GenerationTaskMapping` and `TaskGenerationMapping` interfaces (lines 25-35). Confirmed via grep that these interface names appear only in their own definitions -- they are never referenced anywhere else in the codebase. The `Task` and `GenerationRow` imports remain because they are still used by `enhanceGenerationsWithTaskData`.

### 4. Optimistic update casts to `Record<string, unknown>` -- Fixed
**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useGenerationMutations.ts`

In `useToggleGenerationStar`'s `onMutate` handler:

<result>TypeScript compilation passes with zero errors. Here is a summary of all five fixes:

---

## Fix 1: `extractTaskId` ignores plain string

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/generationTransformers.ts`

The function's type signature accepted `string[] | string | null | undefined`, but a plain `string` fell through the `Array.isArray` check and returned `null`. Added `if (typeof tasks === 'string') return tasks;` before the array check:

```ts
function extractTaskId(tasks: string[] | string | null | undefined): string | null {
  if (typeof tasks === 'string') return tasks;
  if (Array.isArray(tasks) && tasks.length > 0) {
    return tasks[0];
  }
  return null;
}
```

## Fix 2: `PendingClipAction.type` field removed

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/join-clips/lib/clipInitService.ts`

The `type: 'fill' | 'append'` field was set in `consumePendingJoinClips` but never read by `applyPendingClipActions` (which always uses position-based empty-slot-first logic regardless). Removed:
- The `type` field from the `PendingClipAction` interface
- The `type` assignment and now-dead `filledCount` variable from `consumePendingJoinClips`
- All `type` properties from test action objects in `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/tools/join-clips/lib/__tests__/clipInitService.test.ts`

## Fix 3: `VisibilityManager` unused singleton deleted

**Deleted files:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/VisibilityManager.ts` (268 lines)
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/__tests__/VisibilityManager.test.ts`

**Updated file:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/types/browser-extensions.d.ts`
- Removed the `__VIS_CHANGE_AT__` structural global declaration (set only by the deleted VisibilityManager)
- Removed the `__VISIBILITY_MANAGER__` debug global type declaration (which imported from the deleted file)

## Fix 4: LoRA validation duplication extracted

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/imageGeneration.ts`

Extracted a shared `validateLoras(loras)` helper that encapsulates the identical LoRA validation loop (path required, strength between 0-2). Both `validateImageGenerationParams` and `validateBatchImageGenerationParams` now call this single helper instead of duplicating the logic.

<result>TypeScript type check passed with no errors. Here is a summary of all the changes made:

---

**File 1: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ActiveLoRAsDisplay.tsx`**

Replaced 2 occurrences of `bg-slate-50/50 dark:bg-slate-800/30` with `bg-muted/50`:

- **Line 52** (empty state container): `bg-muted/50` -- the empty "None selected" card background
- **Line 68** (LoRA item card): `bg-muted/50` -- the individual LoRA card background

The `bg-muted` token maps to `--muted: 120 20% 80%` (Sage) in light mode and `--muted: 200 10% 20%` (Blue-gray) in dark mode, which provides appropriate subtle contrast in both themes without needing the `dark:` prefix pattern.

---

**File 2: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGalleryItem/components/ShotActions.tsx`**

Three replacements:

- **Line 154**: `bg-green-500 hover:bg-green-600 !text-white` changed to `bg-emerald-500 hover:bg-emerald-600` -- the success/confirmation state when an image was just added to a shot. Removed `!text-white` since the base class already sets `text-white` and `emerald-500` has sufficient contrast with white text.

- **Line 156**: `bg-gray-500/60 hover:bg-gray-600/70 !text-white` changed to `bg-muted/60 hover:bg-muted/70 text-foreground` -- the "already positioned in this shot" state. Uses the semantic `muted` background with `text-foreground` for proper theme adaptation.

- **Line 197**: `bg-gray-500/80 hover:bg-gray-600/90 text-white` changed to `bg-muted/80 hover:bg-muted/90 text-foreground` -- the "already associated without position" state on the small secondary button. Same semantic pattern as above.

Note: The `bg-black/50 hover:bg-black/70 text-white` base classes on both overlay buttons and the shot selector trigger were left unchanged since `black` with opacity is a standard overlay pattern for buttons that float on top of images/media (not a theme color that should change between light/dark modes).</result>

Light mode (`:root`):

```css
--retro: 42 22% 88%;                    /* Warm cream bg #e8e4db */
--retro-hover: 42 14% 82%;             /* Darker cream hover #d8d4cb */
--retro-border: 180 13% 48%;           /* Teal border #6a8a8a */
--retro-foreground: 180 15% 42%;       /* Deeper teal text #5a7a7a */
```

Dark mode (`.dark`):

```css
--retro-hover: 180 24% 23%;             /* Darker teal hover #2d4a4a */
--retro-border: 180 7% 57%;             /* Lighter teal border #8a9a9a */
--retro-foreground: 42 14% 82%;         /* Warm cream text #d8d4cb */

```

```ts
retro: {
    DEFAULT: 'hsl(var(--retro))',
    hover: 'hsl(var(--retro-hover))',
    border: 'hsl(var(--retro-border))',
    foreground: 'hsl(var(--retro-foreground))',
```

**`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/ui/button.tsx`** -- Replaced all hardcoded hex colors in the `retro` and `retro-secondary` variants with the new semantic Tailwind classes (`bg-retro`, `hover:bg-retro-hover`, `border-retro-border`, `text-retro-foreground`) and CSS variable-based shadows (`hsl(var(--retro-shadow)/0.2)`). <result>All three fixes are complete and the TypeScript compiler passes cleanly. Here is a summary of all changes:

---

## Fix 1: IIFE in object spread in imageGeneration.ts

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/tasks/imageGeneration.ts`

Extracted three IIFEs from the `createImageGenerationTask` function into named intermediate variables computed before the object literal:

- **`lorasMap` / `lorasParam`** -- Builds the LoRA map (user-selected LoRAs plus the "in this scene" LoRA for Qwen models) as a plain object, then conditionally wraps it in `{ additional_loras: lorasMap }` or `{}`.
- **`referenceParams`** -- Builds the filtered reference settings via `filterReferenceSettingsByMode()` into a named variable using a simple `if` block instead of a `...(condition && (() => {...})())` IIFE.
- **`hiresLorasOverride`** -- Merges per-phase LoRA overrides with the base `lorasMap`, replacing the nested IIFE that was rebuilding the same LoRA map a second time.

Also extracted the IIFE in `createBatchImageGenerationTasks` into a **`batchReferenceParams`** variable computed once before the `flatMap` loop (it was identical across all iterations anyway, so this is also a minor efficiency win).

The final object literal now simply spreads `...lorasParam`, `...referenceParams`, `...hiresLorasOverride` (and `...batchReferenceParams` in the batch function).

## Fix 2: Duplicated localStorage token reading

**New file:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/supabaseSession.ts`

Created a shared utility with two exported functions:
- `readAccessTokenFromStorage()` -- reads the access token from the Supabase session in localStorage
- `readUserIdFromStorage()` -- reads the user ID from the Supabase session in localStorage

Both share an internal `readRawSession()` helper and `getProjectRef()`, eliminating the duplicated URL parsing and localStorage reading.

**Updated consumers:**
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/taskCreation.ts` -- removed the local `readAccessTokenFromStorage` function (12 lines), now imports from `supabaseSession.ts`. Also removed the now-unused `SUPABASE_URL` import usage for token reading (it's still imported for the edge function URL).
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/invokeWithTimeout.ts` -- removed the local `readAccessTokenFromStorage` function (12 lines), now imports from `supabaseSession.ts`.
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/toolSettingsService.ts` -- removed the local `readUserIdFromStorage` function (12 lines) and the now-unused `SUPABASE_URL` import, now imports from `supabaseSession.ts`.

Note: `createSupabaseClient.ts` was intentionally left with its own copy of `readTokenFromStorage` because it is a bootstrap file that runs before the module system is fully initialized, and the comment in `supabaseSession.ts` documents this.

## Fix 3: useApiKeys upsert pattern

**File:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useApiKeys.ts`

Replaced the manual check-then-insert-or-update pattern (SELECT to check existence, then conditional INSERT or UPDATE -- 3 database round-trips) with a single `.upsert()` call with `{ onConflict: 'id' }`. This is atomic, handles both the insert and update cases in one round-trip, and reduced the `updateApiKeys` function from 24 lines to 9 lines. The `isNotFoundError` import is retained since it's still used by the `fetchApiKeys` function.</result>

<summary>Agent "Fix architecture and init issues" completed</summary>
<result>All three fixes are confirmed correct. Here is a summary of what was changed:

---

**Fix 1: `useDeviceInfo` isTouchDevice stale useMemo**

File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useMobile.tsx`, line 168

The `useMemo` call that computed `isTouchDevice` had a dependency on `isMobile` but `computeIsTouchDevice()` never referenced `isMobile` -- it reads directly from browser APIs (`navigator`, `window`). This meant the value would only recompute when `isMobile` changed, which is an incorrect proxy. The same file already exports a `useIsTouchDevice()` hook that properly listens to `resize` events via `useEffect` and `useState`. The fix replaces:

```tsx
const isTouchDevice = React.useMemo(() => {
  if (typeof window === 'undefined') return !!isMobile;
  return computeIsTouchDevice();
}, [isMobile]);
```

with:

```tsx
const isTouchDevice = useIsTouchDevice();
```

---

**Fix 2: `handleShowTaskDetails` setTimeout hack**

File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGallery/hooks/useMediaGalleryHandlers.ts`, lines 89-91

The `setTimeout(() => { ... }, 100)` wrapping two state updates was a timing hack. React 18+ automatically batches state updates within event handlers, so the `setTimeout` is unnecessary and introduces fragile timing dependencies. The fix calls all three state setters synchronously:

```ts
setSelectedImageForDetails(activeLightboxMedia);
setShowTaskDetailsModal(true);
setActiveLightboxMedia(null);
```

---

**Fix 3: `fetchTaskContext` supabase typed as `unknown`**

File: `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/supabase/functions/complete_task/index.ts`, lines 5 and 38

Added the import (matching the pattern already used in `_shared/auth.ts`):

```ts
import type { SupabaseClient } from 'https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2.39.7';
```

Changed the `fetchTaskContext` parameter from `supabase: unknown` to `supabase: SupabaseClient`. This is the only function in `index.ts` that had `supabase: unknown` -- the `CompleteTaskDeps` interface also uses `unknown` for `createClient` return type and `supabaseAdmin`, but those are intentionally loose for test dependency injection and are out of scope.

---

`npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly with no errors.</result>

---

## Fix 1: Duplicate time formatting -- shared utility

**New file:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/timeFormatting.ts`

This file exports two functions:

- **`formatRelativeDuration(date, options?)`** -- hand-rolls the diff between a `Date` and `Date.now()`, returning abbreviated strings like `"5 mins"`, `"1 hr, 30 mins"`, `"2 days, 3 hrs"`. Accepts an `includeDays` option (default `true`); when `false`, hours accumulate past 24 without rolling over to days. This replaces the duplicated minute/hour/day math that was inlined in `useProcessingTimestamp.ts`.

- **`abbreviateRelativeTime(str)`** -- post-processes a `date-fns` `formatDistanceToNow` string into a shorter form (e.g., `"about 2 hours ago"` becomes `"2 hrs ago"`). This replaces the `defaultAbbreviate` function that was inlined in `useUpdatingTimestamp.ts`.

**Modified file:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useProcessingTimestamp.ts`

The two large formatter functions (`formatProcessingDuration` at ~25 lines and `formatCompletedTime` at ~38 lines) are replaced with one-liners that delegate to `formatRelativeDuration`:

```typescript
const formatProcessingDuration = (startDate: Date): string =>
  `Processing for ${formatRelativeDuration(startDate, { includeDays: false })}`;

const formatCompletedTime = (completedDate: Date): string =>
  `Completed ${formatRelativeDuration(completedDate)} ago`;
```

**Modified file:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/hooks/useUpdatingTimestamp.ts`

The 32-line `defaultAbbreviate` function was removed. Both `useUpdatingTimestamp` and `useTaskTimestamp` now reference `abbreviateRelativeTime` from the shared utility. The unused `React` import was also cleaned up.

---

## Fix 2: Flatten clientThumbnailGenerator promise nesting

**Modified file:** `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/clientThumbnailGenerator.ts`

The `generateClientThumbnail` function previously had three levels of callback nesting (`FileReader.onload` -> `Image.onload` -> `canvas.toBlob`), all wrapped in a single `new Promise()`. This was refactored into:

1. **`readFileAsDataUrl(file: File): Promise<string>`** -- wraps `FileReader` in a promise
2. **`loadImage(dataUrl: string): Promise<HTMLImageElement>`** -- wraps `Image` loading in a promise

The main function is now `async` and reads linearly with `await`:

```typescript
export async function generateClientThumbnail(file, maxSize, quality): Promise<ThumbnailResult> {
  const dataUrl = await readFileAsDataUrl(file);
  const img = await loadImage(dataUrl);
  // ... dimension calculations, canvas drawing ...
  const blob = await new Promise<Blob>((resolve, reject) => {
    canvas.toBlob(/* ... */);
  });
  return { thumbnailBlob: blob, thumbnailWidth, thumbnailHeight, originalWidth, originalHeight };
}
```

The external API (`generateClientThumbnail` and `uploadImageWithThumbnail` signatures and return types) is unchanged, so all callers continue to work without modification.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` passes with zero errors, and all 26 tests across the three affected test files pass.</result>

## Summary of Changes

### 1. Typed Event Bus (`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/lib/typedEvents.ts`) -- NEW FILE

Created a typed event helper with:
- `AppCustomEvents` interface -- single source of truth for all custom event names and their detail shapes
- `dispatchAppEvent()` -- generic function that enforces correct event name and detail type at the call site
- `useAppEventListener()` -- React hook that subscribes/unsubscribes with correct typing, wrapping the boilerplate `useEffect` + `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` pattern

Three events are registered:
- `openSettings`: `{ tab?: string }`
- `selectShotForAddition`: `{ shotId: string; shotName: string }`
- `generation-star-updated`: `{ generationId: string; shotId: string; starred: boolean }`

## Summary

### Problem
`ShotActions` at `/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGalleryItem/components/ShotActions.tsx` had a flat 22-prop interface that mixed unrelated concerns -- shot selector dropdown state, loading/position status flags, quick-create callbacks, and action handlers -- making it hard to reason about.

### Solution
Grouped the 22 individual props into 5 top-level props with named sub-object interfaces:

| Prop | Interface | What it contains | Count |
|------|-----------|-----------------|-------|
| `image` | `GeneratedImageWithMetadata` | The generation row data (unchanged) | 1 |
| `selector` | `ShotSelectorState` | Shot dropdown value, options list, open state, and change callbacks | 6 |
| `status` | `ShotActionStatus` | Mobile flag, video flag, loading image IDs, position booleans, target shot name | 9 |
| `quickCreate` | `ShotQuickCreateState` | Success state object, create handler, success handler | 3 |
| `actions` | `ShotActionCallbacks` | `onCreateShot`, `onNavigateToShot`, `onAddToShot`, `onAddToShotWithoutPosition` | 4 |

### Files changed

1. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGalleryItem/components/ShotActions.tsx`** -- Replaced the flat `ShotActionsProps` interface with 4 new exported sub-interfaces (`ShotSelectorState`, `ShotActionStatus`, `ShotQuickCreateState`, `ShotActionCallbacks`) and a 5-prop `ShotActionsProps`. The component destructures each group at the top of the function body, so all internal logic remains identical.

2. **`/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/src/shared/components/MediaGalleryItem.tsx`** -- Updated the single call site to pass `selector`, `status`, `quickCreate`, and `actions` as grouped objects instead of 22 individual props.

### Verification
`npx tsc --noEmit` completed with zero errors. The component behavior is identical -- this is a pure interface refactor with no logic changes.</result>

Yes, genuinely. Here's what's materially better:

- **process.env.NODE_ENV** (wrong for Vite) → `import.meta.env.DEV` across 8 frontend files
- **Edge functions** using 3 different Deno std versions → all on `0.224.0`
- **Promise callback pyramid** in clientThumbnailGenerator → flat async/await
- **Duplicate time formatting** in two hooks → shared `timeFormatting.ts` utility

**Dead code removed:**
- VisibilityManager (268 lines), deprecated shims (6 files), empty performanceUtils, unused interfaces, unused params/props

**What's NOT just cosmetic:**
- The `createGeneration` variant error was being silently swallowed — now it throws
- `extractTaskId` was dropping plain string task IDs — now handles them
- `useDeviceInfo.isTouchDevice` had stale dependencies — now reactive
- Error messages in complete_task were leaking internals to callers — now sanitized

> DEVELOPER

as in re they improving the code

> AGENT

Honestly, yes — most of them. The strongest wins:

**Actual bugs fixed:** `extractTaskId` dropping strings, `createGeneration` swallowing variant errors, `useDeviceInfo` stale touch detection, error messages leaking internals to API callers

**Real maintainability:** Error files consolidated from 4 scattered locations → 1 directory. 3 copies of localStorage parsing → 1. Typed event bus catches mismatched payloads at compile time instead of silent runtime failures.

**Real readability:** The IIFE-in-object-spread pattern in imageGeneration.ts was genuinely hard to follow. The promise callback pyramid was genuinely nested. The 22-flat-prop ShotActions was genuinely hard to reason about.

**Borderline/cosmetic:** Renaming `pane-positioning` → `panePositioning`, moving test files to `__tests__/`, the retro button CSS variables (they worked fine hardcoded, just violated convention). These are consistency wins, not substance wins.

**One I'd flag as potentially over-engineered:** The typed event bus adds a new abstraction for 3 events. It's better than raw CustomEvents, but a simpler approach (just typing the events inline) would've been fine too.

Net: ~70% genuine improvements, ~30% consistency/convention alignment. No changes that make things worse.

> AGENT

Clean. Here's what I fixed:

**1. Toast z-index** (`sonner.tsx`): `z-[100]` → `z-[100002]`. Task pane is `z-[100001]`, so toasts now render above it.

**2. Task pane placeholder persistence** (`useImageGenSubmit.ts`):
- **Was:** `removeIncomingTask(id)` — just removes the placeholder, doesn't update baselines for any remaining placeholders
- **Now:** `completeIncomingTask(id, processingCount)` — removes the placeholder AND updates remaining placeholders' `baselineCount` to the actual DB processing count (matching the pattern in `useIncomingTaskRunner.ts`)
- **Also:** Now passes `expectedCount: promptCount` and `baselineCount: 0` when creating the placeholder, so the count display is accurate from the start instead of defaulting to `expectedCount: 1`

The root cause was that `useImageGenSubmit` was the only caller using `removeIncomingTask` instead of `completeIncomingTask`. With rapid successive generations, each `removeIncomingTask` call would leave remaining placeholders with stale baselines, causing the count to only increase.

> AGENT

Found it. **Two separate code paths create placeholders for the same generation:**

1. `useSubmitHandler.ts` wraps the call in `runIncomingTask()` → creates **placeholder #1**
2. Inside that execute callback, `useImageGenSubmit.handleNewGenerate()` calls `addIncomingTask()` → creates **placeholder #2**

So every batch generation creates 2 placeholders, double-counts expected tasks, and double-refetches queries. The fix is to strip all incoming task management from `useImageGenSubmit` — the outer `runIncomingTask` wrapper already handles the complete lifecycle.

> DEVELOPER

is this beautifully engineered now?

> AGENT

Honestly? The double-placeholder bug is fixed cleanly, but the broader system is functional rather than beautiful. A few things that are still rough:

**Heuristic matching** — `visibleIncomingTasks` in TaskList.tsx matches placeholders to real tasks by task type + timing window. This works but it's fragile — if two batches of the same type overlap, it can't tell which real task belongs to which placeholder.

**Redundant hiding mechanisms** — Placeholders are hidden in two independent ways: (1) the `visibleIncomingTasks` memo hides them when a matching real task appears in the filtered list, and (2) `completeIncomingTask` formally removes them from state after refetch. Both exist because neither is fully reliable alone.

**Fragile count math** — `cancellableTaskCount` in TasksPane.tsx does `Math.max(dbCount, baseline + totalExpected)` using the oldest placeholder's `baselineCount`. This only works if baselines are updated correctly (which was the bug we just fixed), and it's hard to reason about with overlapping batches.

**60-second stale cleanup** — A timer sweeps placeholders older than 60s every 5s. This is a safety net for when the completion signal is missed — not a sign of confidence in the primary path.

A cleaner architecture would be: `createBatchImageGenerationTasks` returns task IDs → store those IDs on the placeholder → match by exact ID instead of heuristics → remove placeholder when all its task IDs appear in the query cache. No timing windows, no count arithmetic, no stale timers. But that's a bigger refactor than what was needed to fix the immediate bug.
